The current test code for `global_sign_out` only checks against a refresh token action. If you do a sign out and try to access a protected endpoint, like say `get_user(AccessToken)`, it should result to `NotAuthorizedException`. This is not the case as of the current version of moto. I have noticed that all tests against sign out actions are limited to just checking if a refresh token is possible. That should be diversified beyond just a token refresh action.

I have tested my code against actual AWS Cognito without moto, and they do raise the `NotAuthorizedException` when you try to use an access token that has been signed out.

```
# add this to the file: /tests/test_cognitoidp/test_cognitoidp.py

@mock_cognitoidp
def test_global_sign_out():
    conn = boto3.client("cognito-idp", "us-west-2")
    result = user_authentication_flow(conn)

    conn.global_sign_out(AccessToken=result["access_token"])

    with pytest.raises(ClientError) as ex:
        conn.get_user(AccessToken=result["access_token"])

    err = ex.value.response["Error"]
    err["Code"].should.equal("NotAuthorizedException")
```

<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a library management system. Design a class `Book` that models a book in the library. The class should have the following attributes:
- `title`: a string representing the title of the book.
- `author`: a string representing the author of the book.
- `isbn`: a string representing the ISBN number of the book.
- `publication_year`: an integer representing the year the book was published.
- `available`: a boolean indicating whether the book is available for loan.

The class should also include the following methods:
- `check_out_book()`: sets the `available` attribute to `False`.
- `return_book()`: sets the `available` attribute to `True`.
- `is_available()`: returns the value of the `available` attribute.

Additionally, implement a function `find_books_by_author(books_list, author_name)` that takes a list of `Book` objects and an author's name as input and returns a list of all books by that author.

**Sample Input:**
```python
books = [
    Book("1984", "George Orwell", "9780451524935", 1949),
    Book("Animal Farm", "George Orwell", "9780451526342", 1945),
    Book("To Kill a Mockingbird", "Harper Lee", "9780060935467", 1960)
]
author_name = "George Orwell"
```

**Sample Output:**
```python
[
    Book("1984", "George Orwell", "9780451524935", 1949),
    Book("Animal Farm", "George Orwell", "9780451526342", 1945)
]
```

```python
class Book:
    def __init__(self, title, author, isbn, publication_year, available=True):
        """
        Initializes a new Book instance.

        :param title: str - the title of the book
        :param author: str - the author of the book
        :param isbn: str - the ISBN number of the book
        :param publication_year: int - the year the book was published
        :param available: bool - whether the book is available for loan
        """
        self.title = title
        self.author = author
        self.isbn = isbn
        self.publication_year = publication_year
        self.available = available

    def check_out_book(self):
        """
        Marks the book as checked out by setting the available attribute to False.
        """
        self.available = False

    def return_book(self):
        """
        Marks the book as returned by setting the available attribute to True.
        """
        self.available = True

    def is_available(self):
        """
        Returns the availability status of the book.

        :return: bool - True if the book is available, False otherwise
        """
        return self.available


def find_books_by_author(books_list, author_name):
    """
    Finds all books by a given author in the list of books.

    :param books_list: list of Book - the list of books to search through
    :param author_name: str - the name of the author to find books for
    :return: list of Book - the list of books by the specified author
    """
    return [book for book in books_list if book.author == author_name]
```<#sep#> we know that \(-1\) is a quadratic non-residue because the Legendre symbol \( \left( \frac{-1}{p} \right) = (-1)^{(p-1)/2} = -1 \).

3. **Transform the Equation**: Multiplying both sides of \( x^2 \equiv -\frac{p+1}{4} \pmod{p} \) by 4 gives:
   \[
   4x^2 \equiv - (p+1) \pmod{p}
   \]
   Since \( p \equiv 3 \pmod{4} \), we have \( p + 1 \equiv 4 \pmod{4} \), and thus:
   \[
   4x^2 \equiv -4 \pmod{p}
   \]
   Dividing both sides by 4 (since 4 is coprime to \( p \)):
   \[
   x^2 \equiv -1 \pmod{p}
   \]
   Let \( y = 2x \). Then the equation becomes:
   \[
   y^2 \equiv -1 \pmod{p}
   \]

4. **Solutions Analysis**: For \( p \equiv 3 \pmod{4} \), the equation \( y^2 \equiv -1 \pmod{p} \) has no solutions because \(-1\) is a quadratic non-residue modulo \( p \). Therefore, the original equation \( x^2 \equiv -\frac{p+1}{4} \pmod{p} \) also has no solutions, implying \( N(p) = 0 \) for all primes \( p \equiv 3 \pmod{4} \).

5. **Sum Calculation**: The sum \( \sum_{p \leq 100} N(p) \) over all primes \( p \equiv 3 \pmod{4} \) up to 100 is the sum of zeros, resulting in:
   \[
   \sum_{p \leq 100} N(p) = 0
   \]

Thus, the final answer is \(\boxed{0}\).<#sep#> in the TSP path the route must first travel through a country's international as the entrance and exit point, and in between cover all cities.

+<#sep#>You are given a CSV file named `employee_data.csv` containing employee records. Each record includes the employee's name, ID, department, and salary. Your task is to write a Python function `analyze_employee_data` that reads this file and outputs the following information:

- Total number of employees.
- Average salary of all employees.
- Department with the highest number of employees.
- Highest salary in the company.
- Lowest salary in the company.
- List of employees with salaries above the average.

**Input Format:**
The CSV file `employee_data.csv` will have the following columns: `Name`, `ID`, `Department`, `Salary`.

**Sample Input:**
```
Name,ID,Department,Salary
Alice,1,HR,50000
Bob,2,Engineering,75000
Charlie,3,Engineering,80000
David,4,Marketing,45000
Eve,5,HR,55000
```

**Sample Output:**
```
Total number of employees: 5
Average salary: 61000.00
Department with the highest number of employees: Engineering
Highest salary in the company: 80000
Lowest salary in the company: 45000
Employees with salaries above the average: ['Bob', 'Charlie']
```

```python
import csv
from collections import defaultdict

def analyze_employee_data(csv_file):
    """
    Analyzes employee data from a CSV file and prints various statistics.

    Parameters:
    csv_file (str): The path to the CSV file containing employee data.

    Returns:
    None
    """
    employees = []
    salary_sum = 0
    department_count = defaultdict(int)
    highest_salary = float('-inf')
    lowest_salary = float('inf')
    
    with open(csv_file, mode='r') as file:
        reader = csv.DictReader(file)
        for row in reader:
            name = row['Name']
            employee_id = row['ID']
            department = row['Department']
            salary = float(row['Salary'])
            
            employees.append({'Name': name, 'ID': employee_id, 'Department': department, 'Salary': salary})
            salary_sum += salary
            department_count[department] += 1
            
            highest_salary = max(highest_salary, salary)
            lowest_salary = min(lowest_salary, salary)
    
    total_employees = len(employees)
    average_salary = salary_sum / total_employees if total_employees > 0 else 0.0
    
    department_with_most_employees = max(department_count, key=department_count.get) if department_count else None
    
    employees_above_average = [emp for emp in employees if emp['Salary'] > average_salary]
        
    print(f"Total number of employees: {total_employees}")
    print(f"Average salary: {average_salary:.2f}")
    print(f"Department with the highest number of employees: {department_with_most_employees}")
    print(f"Highest salary in the company: {highest_salary}")
    print(f"Lowest salary in the company: {lowest_salary}")
    print(f"Employees with salaries above the average: {[emp['Name'] for emp in employees_above_average]}")

<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Book` class in Python. The class should have the following attributes: `title`, `author`, `publication_year`, `isbn`, `pages`, and `copies_available`. Implement the following methods:

- `add_copies(number_of_copies)`: Adds the specified number of copies to the book's available stock. Ensure that the number of copies to add is positive.
- `remove_copies(number_of_copies)`: Removes the specified number of copies from the book's available stock. Ensure that the number of copies to remove is positive and does not exceed the available stock.
- `update_copies(new_copies)`: Updates the total number of available copies directly. Ensure that the number of copies is non-negative.
- `display_info()`: Displays the book information in a formatted manner.

**Sample Input/Output:**

```
Input:
book = Book("The Great Gatsby", "F. Scott Fitzgerald", 1925, "9780915799134", 180, 5)
book.display_info()
book.add_copies(2)
book.display_info()
book.remove_copies(3)
book.display_info()
book.update_copies(10)
book.display_info()

Output:
Title: The Great Gatsby
Author: F. Scott Fitzgerald
Publication Year: 1925
ISBN: 9780915799134
Pages: 180
Copies Available: 5
Title: The Great Gatsby
Author: F. Scott Fitzgerald
Publication Year: 1925
ISBN: 9780915799134
Pages: 180
Copies Available: 7
Title: The Great Gatsby
Author: F. Scott Fitzgerald
Publication Year: 1925
ISBN: 9780915799134
Pages: 180
Copies Available: 4
Title: The Great Gatsby
Author: F. Scott Fitzgerald
Publication Year: 1925
ISBN: 9780915799134
Pages: 180
Copies Available: 10
```

```python
class Book:
    def __init__(self, title, author, publication_year, isbn, pages, copies_available):
        """Initialize a new Book instance."""
        self.title = title
        self.author = author
        self.publication_year = publication_year
        self.isbn = isbn
        self.pages = pages
        self.copies_available = copies_available

    def add_copies(self, number_of_copies):
        """Add copies to the book's available stock."""
        if number_of_copies > 0:
            self.copies_available += number_of_copies
        else:
            print("Number of copies to add must be positive.")

    def remove_copies(self, number_of_copies):
        """Remove copies from the book's available stock."""
        if number_of_copies > 0 and self.copies_available >= number_of_copies:
            self.copies_available -= number_of_copies
        elif number_of_copies > self.copies_available:
            print("Not enough copies available to remove.")
        else:
            print("Number of copies to remove must be positive.")

    def update_copies(self, new_copies):
        """Update the total number of available copies directly."""
        if new_copies >= 0:
            self.copies_available = new_copies
        else:
            print("Number of copies must be non-negative.")

    def display_info(self):
        """Display the book information in a formatted manner."""
        print(f"Title: {self.title}")
        print(f"Author: {self.author}")
        print(f"Publication Year: {self.publication_year}")
        print(f"ISBN: {self.isbn}")
        print(f"Pages: {self.pages}")
        print(f"Copies Available: {self.copies_available}")

(D + X)/2 = A + r(C - A)

We can express X from both equations and set them equal:

From first equation: X = 2B + 2s(D - B) - C

From second equation: X = 2A + 2r(C - A) - D

2B + 2s(D - B) - C = 2A + 2r(C - A) - D

2B - C + 2s(D - B) = 2A - D + 2r(C - A)

2B - C - 2A + D + 2s(D - B) - 2r(C - A) = 0

Let’s expand the terms:

2B - C - 2A + D + 2sD - 2sB - 2rC + 2rA = 0

Group similar terms:

(2B - 2sB) + (-C - 2rC) + (D + 2sD) + (-2A + 2rA) = 0

Factor:

B(2 - 2s) + C(-1 - 2r) + D(1 + 2s) + A(-2 + 2r) = 0

<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Calculator` class in Python that supports basic arithmetic operations: addition, subtraction, multiplication, and division. The class should include methods for each operation. Each method should validate its inputs to ensure they are either integers or floats. If the inputs are invalid, the method should raise a `ValueError`. For the division method, if the divisor is zero, a `ZeroDivisionError` should be raised.

**Sample Input/Output:**
- Input: `calc = Calculator(); calc.add(10, 5)`
- Output: `15`
- Input: `calc = Calculator(); calc.divide(10, 0)`
- Output: Raises `ZeroDivisionError` with message "Cannot divide by zero"
- Input: `calc = Calculator(); calc.multiply(3, 'a')`
- Output: Raises `ValueError` with message "Inputs must be integers or floats"

```python
class Calculator:
    def add(self, a, b):
        """
        Adds two numbers and returns the result.
        
        :param a: First number (int or float)
        :param b: Second number (int or float)
        :return: Sum of a and b
        :raises ValueError: If inputs are not integers or floats
        """
        if not isinstance(a, (int, float)) or not isinstance(b, (int, float)):
            raise ValueError("Inputs must be integers or floats")
        return a + b

    def subtract(self, a, b):
        """
        Subtracts the second number from the first and returns the result.
        
        :param a: First number (int or float)
        :param b: Second number (int or float)
        :return: Difference of a and b
        :raises ValueError: If inputs are not integers or floats
        """
        if not isinstance(a, (int, float)) or not isinstance(b, (int, float)):
            raise ValueError("Inputs must be integers or floats")
        return a - b

    def multiply(self, a, b):
        """
        Multiplies two numbers and returns the result.
        
        :param a: First number (int or float)
        :param b: Second number (int or float)
        :return: Product of a and b
        :raises ValueError: If inputs are not integers or floats
        """
        if not isinstance(a, (int, float)) or not isinstance(b, (int, float)):
            raise ValueError("Inputs must be integers or floats")
        return a * b

    def divide(self, a, b):
        """
        Divides the first number by the second and returns the result.
        
        :param a: First number (int or float)
        :param b: Second number (int or float)
        :return: Quotient of a and b
        :raises ValueError: If inputs are not integers or floats
        :raises ZeroDivisionError: If the second number is zero
        """
        if not isinstance(a, (int, float)) or not isinstance(b, (int, float)):
            raise ValueError("Inputs must be integers or floats")
        if b == 0:
            raise ZeroDivisionError("Cannot divide by zero")
        return a / b

<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Book` class for a library management system. The class should encapsulate the following attributes: `title`, `author`, `isbn`, `publisher`, `year_published`, `language`, `page_count`, `available_copies`, and `genres`. The `genres` attribute should be a list of genres associated with the book.

Implement the `Book` class with the following methods:
- `is_available()`: Returns `True` if there is at least one available copy of the book, otherwise returns `False`.
- `check_out_book()`: Decreases the `available_copies` by one if the book is available. If the book is not available, print the message "Error: Book is not available for check out."
- `return_book()`: Increases the `available_copies` by one.
- `add_genre(genre)`: Adds a new genre to the `genres` list if it is not already present.

**Sample Input/Output:**
```python
book = Book("1984", "George Orwell", "9780451524935", "Signet Classic", 1950, "English", 328, 3, ["Dystopian", "Political Fiction"])
print(book.is_available())  # Output: True
book.check_out_book()
print(book.available_copies)  # Output: 2
book.return_book()
print(book.available_copies)  # Output: 3
book.add_genre("Science Fiction")
print(book.genres)  # Output: ['Dystopian', 'Political Fiction', 'Science Fiction']
book.add_genre("Dystopian")
print(book.genres)  # Output: ['Dystopian', 'Political Fiction', 'Science Fiction']
```

```python
class Book:
    def __init__(self, title, author, isbn, publisher, year_published, language, page_count, available_copies, genres=None):
        """
        Initializes a new Book instance with the given attributes.
        
        :param title: The title of the book.
        :param author: The author of the book.
        :param isbn: The ISBN of the book.
        :param publisher: The publisher of the book.
        :param year_published: The year the book was published.
        :param language: The language the book is written in.
        :param page_count: The number of pages in the book.
        :param available_copies: The number of available copies of the book.
        :param genres: A list of genres associated with the book.
        """
        self.title = title
        self.author = author
        self.isbn = isbn
        self.publisher = publisher
        self.year_published = year_published
        self.language = language
        self.page_count = page_count
        self.available_copies = available_copies
        self.genres = genres if genres is not None else []

    def is_available(self):
        """
        Checks if the book is available for check out.
        
        :return: True if the book is available, False otherwise.
        """
        return self.available_copies > 0

    def check_out_book(self):
        """
        Checks out a book if it is available, otherwise prints an error message.
        """
        if self.is_available():
            self.available_copies -= 1
        else:
            print("Error: Book is not available for check out.")

    def return_book(self):
        """
        Returns a book, increasing the number of available copies by one.
        """
        self.available_copies += 1

    def add_genre(self, genre):
        """
        Adds a new genre to the book's genres list if it is not already present.
        
        :param genre: The genre to be added.
        """
        if genre not in self.genres:
            self.genres.append(genre)

x = 0 + r C_x

y = 0 + r C_y

((D_x + t)/2, D_y/2) = (r C_x, r C_y)

Thus,

Alternatively, the starting point can be considered as part of the path's first step. To model this, we start with a begin state where mask has one country visited and current_airport is its exit. Then, after going around all countries, we connect the final exit back to the first entry.

But this is difficult to handle.

Alternatively，注意我们要求的是一个循环，所以可以强制 trip starts and ends at any arbitrary initial international airport. 我们可以最小化针对所有可能的起始点的总成本。

对此，我们可以将问题转换为找到一个循环，访问所有 countries 的路径，其中我们枚舉起始的 entry 国际机场，计算 the cost of the minimal such loop starting and ending there. 然後選取minimal one over all starting airports.

这样， the DP can then be computed for each starting airport separately.

可以这样：

遍历每一个 international airport as the starting point:

   每次 the trip starts there.

   the first step is to choose a country that contains this airport as an entry.

进入这个 country，然后走它的 path，退出來到某 airport, and the rest of the countries must be visited.

Thus， the DP for each starting point can be:

   mask is 0 except for starting country (if the first step is leaving the starting country?)

No， the first step must be to enter the country through its entry, then exit, and so on.

因此，我们需要建立一个动态规划，对于给定的起点 airport 建立 state.

But how does that help?

这可能会太慢，因为 there are up to 15 starting points.

. Let's see:

步骤：

1. 预处理所有country的 entry-exit对路径的最短成本.

2. 现在建立 DPs for all possible start airport.

3. 然後 the minimal of all possible such minimum costs is the answer.

Even if S is 15, 这 requires doing 15 DP runs. 这 是可以的.

Thus， the approach would be:

Statement:

For each possible starting airport (国际 airport节点u in all countries):

   run a DP to find the minimum path that starts at u, visits all countries, ends at some node，然后 there must be a flight back from the final airport to u. 正确吗?

Wait， the trip must be a cycle, so the start and end must be the same airport u.

So for the DP starting at u:

We need to end at u 通过 the final route, which costs flight between the last country's exit and u 的 entry.

So the total cost for starting at u would be the sum of:

   所有 countries' paths' costs,

   plus the flights between the transitions,

   plus the return flight from the final exit airport back to u.

于是：

For each starting airport u:

   the initial state is mask = 0 and current_pos = u (before any country has been visited). 

但是, first step must be to enter a country through one of its entry points 这 must be one whose entry point could be reached by a flight from nowhere, 但 this approach cannot be  done since you need to come from a previous country.

 此 setup is incorrect.

Hmm, perhaps the correct initial state is that we are at airport u, and we need to choose to first visit one of the possible countries connected 通過 u.

 即：

初始化 the DP as follows：

for all countries that contains a entry airport (entry_node) with flight from u到 entry_node? No, 因为 you can enter a country by opportunistic choice of the entry and<#sep#>Feature request: Implement IAM back end part of update_assume_role_policy to validate policies

<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a class `Employee` to manage employee data in a company's HR system. The class should encapsulate the following attributes: `employee_id`, `name`, `position`, `department`, `salary`, `hire_date`, and `performance_rating`. Your implementation should include a constructor to initialize these attributes and the following methods:

- `apply_raise(raise_percentage)`: Increases the `salary` by the given percentage.
- `change_department(new_department)`: Updates the `department` attribute to the new department name.
- `promote(new_position)`: Updates the `position` attribute to the new position name and increases the `salary` by 20%.
- `update_performance_rating(rating)`: Updates the `performance_rating` attribute to the new rating.

**Sample Input/Output:**

```python
# Create an employee
emp = Employee(1, "John Doe", "Software Engineer", "Engineering", 75000, "2021-06-01", 4.5)

# Apply a 10% raise
emp.apply_raise(10)
print(emp.salary)  # Output: 82500.0

# Change department
emp.change_department("Product")
print(emp.department)  # Output: Product

# Promote employee
emp.promote("Senior Software Engineer")
print(emp.position)  # Output: Senior Software Engineer
print(emp.salary)  # Output: 99000.0

# Update performance rating
emp.update_performance_rating(5.0)
print(emp.performance_rating)  # Output: 5.0
```

```python
class Employee:
    def __init__(self, employee_id, name, position, department, salary, hire_date, performance_rating):
        """
        Initializes an Employee object with the given attributes.

        :param employee_id: Unique identifier for the employee.
        :param name: Name of the employee.
        :param position: Current position of the employee.
        :param department: Department where the employee works.
        :param salary: Current salary of the employee.
        :param hire_date: Date when the employee was hired.
        :param performance_rating: Performance rating of the employee.
        """
        self.employee_id = employee_id
        self.name = name
        self.position = position
        self.department = department
        self.salary = salary
        self.hire_date = hire_date
        self.performance_rating = performance_rating

    def apply_raise(self, raise_percentage):
        """
        Increases the employee's salary by the given percentage.

        :param raise_percentage: Percentage by which to increase the salary.
        """
        self.salary += self.salary * (raise_percentage / 100)

    def change_department(self, new_department):
        """
        Updates the employee's department to the new department name.

        :param new_department: New department name.
        """
        self.department = new_department

    def promote(self, new_position):
        """
        Promotes the employee to a new position and increases the salary by 20%.

        :param new_position: New position name.
        """
        self.position = new_position
        self.apply_raise(20)

    def update_performance_rating(self, rating):
        """
        Updates the employee's performance rating to the new rating.

        :param rating: New performance rating.
        """
        self.performance_rating = rating

 for current state (mask, current):

Perhaps the way to handle the循环 is to consider that when all countries are visited, the current position is v， and we need to add the cost of the flight from v back to u. 

 Thus, the total cost for starting at u is the cost of the DP state(mask=all, v) + the flight from v到 u. 

 Thus, for each starting airport u:

   compute the minimal over all v of ( DP_end_value(u的 start) + flight_cost[v][u] )

如果 there is no flight between v and u, then this path is invalid.

 Thus，在 the DP for a given starting airport u， the minimal total cost is the minimal of [DP[mask=all][v] + flightcost between v and u], provided that flight between v and u exists.

 Thus the overall approach is manageable.

这就 gives a plan.

现在， the steps in code are:

First, preprocess all countries:

For each country c:

   collect the list of cities.

   create its adjacency list / or matrix for the internal flights.

   precompute for all pairs of entries and exits（international cities of this country's) the minimal path cost征 to visit all cities in the country as TSPP.

   记录 these in a二维table for起始和结尾.

   also ensure that for this country, at least one path exists from 啊n entry to exit covering all cities.

If any country has no这样的 paths, 标记 the problem is impossible.

Second:

预处理国际 airports之间 flight 的 adjacency matrix.

以 the unique ID's of the 和际 airports (S up to15) as the vets，建立 adjacency list 或 matrix between them.

第三：

制作 the DP for each starting airport u (国际 airport).

For each such u:

   初始化 the DP table as all infinity.

   初始化的 DP的 initial state: mask=0， current =u.

   The cost of this state is 0 ( initially, no paths done yet).

   将 the DP start state c设为0.

然后， the transitions proceed as:

for each mask in 0... (all bits except all):

   for each current_position in all airports:

       如果 the current's DP mask unrecognized 影响, continua.

                   其 cost is the flight_cost between current_pos's position and s.

                   plus the country's internal cost(s 进入到 e exit).

                   the total cost is 这. 

                   the new mask is mask |该 country的 bit.

                   the new current position 是e.

                   we can update the new DP state (new_mask, e) with the minimal cost.

https://github.com/getmoto/moto/blob/a7ce969b260ac87604d7b87a171d8ce5d3c7b60c/moto/ec2/models/transit_gateway_route_tables.py#L31

FROM:
```
        self.route_table_association: Dict[str, str] = {}
        self.route_table_propagation: Dict[str, str] = {}
```

TO:
```
        self.route_table_association: Dict[str, str] = {}
        self.route_table_propagation: Dict[str, str] = [{}]
```

Object should be "appended" instead of current static assignment...

FROM:
```
(...).propagation = ...
```

TO
`
.... .propagation.append( [ {
  "state": "enable",
  "TransitGatewayRouteTableId: "tgw-attach-xxxxxxxx"
 } ] 
)
`

Important note:
Seams like currently propagations are being handled by moto the same way as associations, i feel it shouldnt.
Attach-Associations, have a 1 to 1 relation with route tables.
Attach-Propagations, have a N to 1 relation with route tables.

The same way the TGW Peering-Attachments may be propagated to many routing tables: https://github.com/getmoto/moto/blob/a7ce969b260ac87604d7b87a171d8ce5d3c7b60c/moto/ec2/models/transit_gateway_attachments.py#L321


--- 

AWS:
`
aws ec2 get-transit-gateway-route-table-propagations --transit-gateway-route-table-id tgw-rtb-xxxxxxxx 
`

"get-transit-gateway-route-table-propagations" should list all "tgw-attach-xxxxxxxx" propagated that exist in a routing table.


**Current Behaviour:**
`
aws ec2 enable-transit-gateway-route-table-propagation --transit-gateway-attachment-id tgw-attach-xxxxxx  --transit-gateway-route-table-id tgw-rtb-xxxxxx
`

When we enable propagations (add new "tgw-attach-xxxxxxxx" to a route table), only the last one is saved in Moto.

**Expected behaviour:**
1. enable-transit-gateway-route-table-propagation  and disable, will add/remove attachments "tgw-attach-xxxxxxxx" from a specific tgw route table.
2. get-transit-gateway-route-table-propagations should list all enabled propagations (attachments propagated in a TgwRT)

<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Book` class to manage library books. The class should support the following functionalities:

- **Initialization**: A book is initialized with a title, author, ISBN, publication year, and the number of available copies.
- **Check Out**: Decrease the number of available copies by 1, ensuring it does not go below 0.
- **Return Book**: Increase the number of available copies by 1.
- **Get Info**: Return a formatted string containing the book's title, author, and publication year.
- **Compare Books**: A static method to compare two books based on their ISBNs and return `True` if they are the same, otherwise `False`.

**Sample Input/Output**:
- Initialize a book with title "1984", author "George Orwell", ISBN "9780451524935", publication year 1949, and 3 available copies.
- Call `check_out()` twice, then `return_book()` once.
- Call `get_info()` to get the book's information.
- Compare this book with another book having the same ISBN and another with a different ISBN.

```python
class Book:
    def __init__(self, title, author, ISBN, publication_year, available_copies):
        """
        Initialize a new book with the given details.

        :param title: The title of the book.
        :param author: The author of the book.
        :param ISBN: The International Standard Book Number of the book.
        :param publication_year: The year the book was published.
        :param available_copies: The number of copies available in the library.
        """
        self.title = title
        self.author = author
        self.ISBN = ISBN
        self.publication_year = publication_year
        self.available_copies = available_copies

    def check_out(self):
        """
        Decrease the number of available copies by 1, ensuring it does not go below 0.
        """
        if self.available_copies > 0:
            self.available_copies -= 1

    def return_book(self):
        """
        Increase the number of available copies by 1.
        """
        self.available_copies += 1

    def get_info(self):
        """
        Return a formatted string containing the book's title, author, and publication year.

        :return: A string with the book's title, author, and publication year.
        """
        return f"'{self.title}' by {self.author}, published in {self.publication_year}"

    @staticmethod
    def compare_books(book1, book2):
        """
        Compare two books based on their ISBNs.

        :param book1: The first book to compare.
        :param book2: The second book to compare.
        :return: True if the books have the same ISBN, False otherwise.
        """
        return book1.ISBN == book2.ISBN

= k² [ (16 - 16k + 4k²)/(1 + k)^2 + (8 - 4k)/(1 + k) ]

= k² [ (16 - 16k + 4k² + (8 - 4k)(1 + k)) / (1 + k)^2 ]

Expand (8 - 4k)(1 + k) = 8(1) + 8k -4k(1) -4k² = 8 + 8k -4k -4k² = 8 +4k -4k²

So numerator inside the brackets:

16 -16k +4k² +8 +4k -4k² = 16 +8 -16k +4k +4k² -4k² = 24 -12k

Therefore,

k² * (24 -12k)/(1 + k)^2 = 12 k² (2 - k)/(1 + k)^2

Putting it all together, the equation becomes:

C_x [2*(1 -4k + k²)/(1 + k)] + C_y [2 f (k - 1)] + 12 k² (2 - k)/(1 + k)^2 = 0

C_x² + C_y² - 2 C_x + 2 f C_y = 0

Which can be rearranged to:

C_x² + C_y² = 2 C_x - 2 f C_y

@pytest.fixture(scope="function")
def aws_credentials():
    """Mocked AWS Credentials for moto."""
    os.environ["AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID"] = "testing"
    os.environ["AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY"] = "testing"
    os.environ["AWS_SECURITY_TOKEN"] = "testing"

    os.environ["AWS_DEFAULT_REGION"] = "us-east-1"

def sm_client(aws_credentials):
    with mock_aws():
        yield boto3.client("secretsmanager", region_name="us-east-1")

            return event
        '''

    zip_file.writestr('lambda_function.py', code)
    zip_file.close()
    zip_output.seek(0)
    return zip_output.read()


# create mocked lambda with zip file

When i debugged the code I see that [get-backend](https://github.com/getmoto/moto/blob/master/moto/awslambda/utils.py#L43) correctly selects the `LambdaSimpleBackend`. 
However, secretmanager invokes the lambda using an [overload](https://github.com/getmoto/moto/blob/master/moto/secretsmanager/models.py#L767) of `invoke` that is not implemented in [LambdaSimpleBackend](https://github.com/getmoto/moto/blob/master/moto/awslambda_simple/models.py#L7). 
It ends up calling [this implementation](https://github.com/getmoto/moto/blob/master/moto/awslambda/models.py#L1087) instead.

<#sep#>You are given a list of `N` sentences, each represented as a string. Your task is to compute the TF-IDF (Term Frequency-Inverse Document Frequency) scores for each word in each sentence. The TF-IDF score for a word in a sentence is calculated as the product of its term frequency (TF) and inverse document frequency (IDF). The term frequency of a word in a sentence is the number of times the word appears in the sentence divided by the total number of words in the sentence. The inverse document frequency of a word is the logarithm of the total number of sentences divided by the number of sentences containing the word plus one. 

Write a function `tf_idf(sentences)` that takes a list of sentences as input and returns a list of dictionaries. Each dictionary corresponds to a sentence and contains words as keys and their respective TF-IDF scores as values.

**Constraints:**
- 1 ≤ N ≤ 100
- Each sentence contains at most 100 words.
- Words consist of lowercase and uppercase English letters and spaces.

**Sample Input:**
```python
["Python is a powerful programming language.", 
 "Python can be used for web development.", 
 "Python is often used for data analysis."]
```

**Sample Output:**
```python
{'python': 0.08701137698962972, 'is': 0.28768207245178085, 'a': 0.28768207245178085, 'powerful': 0.28768207245178085, 'programming': 0.28768207245178085, 'language.': 0.28768207245178085}
{'python': 0.08701137698962972, 'can': 0.28768207245178085, 'be': 0.28768207245178085, 'used': 0.19178309361166502, 'for': 0.19178309361166502, 'web': 0.28768207245178085, 'development.': 0.28768207245178085}
{'python': 0.08701137698962972, 'is': 0.28768207245178085, 'often': 0.28768207245178085, 'used': 0.19178309361166502, 'for': 0.19178309361166502, 'data': 0.28768207245178085, 'analysis.': 0.28768207245178085}
```

```python
import math
from collections import defaultdict

def tf_idf(sentences):
    """
    Computes the TF-IDF scores for each word in each sentence.
    
    Parameters:
    sentences (list of str): A list of sentences.
    
    Returns:
    list of dict: A list of dictionaries where each dictionary contains words as keys and their TF-IDF scores as values.
    """
    def compute_tf(word_freq, total_words):
        return {word: freq / total_words for word, freq in word_freq.items()}
    
    def compute_idf(word_freq_across_docs, corpus_size):
        return {word: math.log(corpus_size / (num_docs + 1)) for word, num_docs in word_freq_across_docs.items()}
    
    corpus_size = len(sentences)
    word_freq_across_docs = defaultdict(int)
    sentences_word_freq = []

    for sentence in sentences:
        words = sentence.lower().split()
        total_words = len(words)
        word_freq = defaultdict(int)

        for word in words:
            word_freq[word] += 1
            word_freq_across_docs[word] += 1
        
        sentences_word_freq.append((word_freq, total_words))

    idfs = compute_idf(word_freq_across_docs, corpus_size)

    tfidf_scores = []
    for word_freq, total_words in sentences_word_freq:
        tfs = compute_tf(word_freq, total_words)
        tfidf_scores.append({word: tfs[word] * idfs[word] for word in word_freq})

    return tfidf_scores

# Test the function
sentences = ["Python is a powerful programming language.", 
             "Python can be used for web development.", 
             "Python is often used for data analysis."]
tfidf_result = tf_idf(sentences)

# Print the TF-IDF scores
for sentence_scores in tfidf_result:
    print(sentence_scores)

.This is the crux of the problem. How to compute for each country the minimal path between entry and exit？

 The tsp path dp for each country c:

初始化：

 dp[mask][current_node] = the minimal cost to visit the nodes in the mask, ending at current node.

 initiate all to infinite.

 for each node in the country:

   dp[1<<node节点][node] =0.

then for each mask in all possible masks except full:

   for each current_node:

      if dp[Mask][current_node] is not infinite:

          for 羁束 all nodes not in mask:

              if there is an edge between current_node和 the next_node:

                  new_mask = mask | (1<<next_node)

                  new_cost = dp[mask][current_node] +边的 cost.

                  if this is better than the existing DP[new_mask][next_node], update it.

最终， for each entry 和 exit possible in the country's国际 airports:

 the minimal path cost between them visited all nodes是 the DP[full_mask]. [exit].

 这样， the precomputing for a country of size M_c 的 time is O(2^M_c * M_c^2).

<#sep#>You are tasked with designing a RESTful API using Flask to manage a collection of books. The API must support the following operations:

1. **GET /books**: Retrieve a list of all books.
2. **GET /books/{book_id}**: Retrieve a specific book by its unique identifier.
3. **POST /books**: Add a new book to the collection.
4. **PUT /books/{book_id}**: Update an existing book's details by its unique identifier.
5. **DELETE /books/{book_id}**: Remove a book from the collection by its unique identifier.

Each book is represented by the following attributes:
- `id` (int): A unique identifier for the book.
- `title` (string): The title of the book.
- `author` (string): The author of the book.
- `published_year` (int): The year the book was published.

**Sample Input/Output:**

- **GET /books**
  - Response: `[
      {"id": 1, "title": "1984", "author": "George Orwell", "published_year": 1949},
      {"id": 2, "title": "The Great Gatsby", "author": "F. Scott Fitzgerald", "published_year": 1925},
      {"id": 3, "title": "To Kill a Mockingbird", "author": "Harper Lee", "published_year": 1960}
    ]`

- **GET /books/1**
  - Response: `{"id": 1, "title": "1984", "author": "George Orwell", "published_year": 1949}`

- **POST /books**
  - Request Body: `{"title": "Brave New World", "author": "Aldous Huxley", "published_year": 1932}`
  - Response: `{"id": 4, "title": "Brave New World", "author": "Aldous Huxley", "published_year": 1932}`

- **PUT /books/1**
  - Request Body: `{"title": "Nineteen Eighty-Four"}`
  - Response: `{"id": 1, "title": "Nineteen Eighty-Four", "author": "George Orwell", "published_year": 1949}`

- **DELETE /books/1**
  - Response: `{"result": true}`

```python
from flask import Flask, jsonify, request, abort

app = Flask(__name__)

# Sample data representing a list of books
books = [
    {'id': 1, 'title': '1984', 'author': 'George Orwell', 'published_year': 1949},
    {'id': 2, 'title': 'The Great Gatsby', 'author': 'F. Scott Fitzgerald', 'published_year': 1925},
    {'id': 3, 'title': 'To Kill a Mockingbird', 'author': 'Harper Lee', 'published_year': 1960}
]

def find_book(book_id):
    """Helper function to find a book by its ID."""
    return next((book for book in books if book['id'] == book_id), None)

@app.route('/books', methods=['GET'])
def get_books():
    """Retrieve a list of all books."""
    return jsonify(books)

@app.route('/books/<int:book_id>', methods=['GET'])
def get_book(book_id):
    """Retrieve a specific book by its ID."""
    book = find_book(book_id)
    if book is None:
        abort(404)
    return jsonify(book)

@app.route('/books', methods=['POST'])
def add_book():
    """Add a new book to the list."""
    if not request.json or 'title' not in request.json or 'author' not in request.json or 'published_year' not in request.json:
        abort(400)
    new_book = {
        'id': books[-1]['id'] + 1 if books else 1,
        'title': request.json['title'],
        'author': request.json['author'],
        'published_year': request.json['published_year']
    }
    books.append(new_book)
    return jsonify(new_book), 201

@app.route('/books/<int:book_id>', methods=['PUT'])
def update_book(book_id):
    """Update an existing book by its ID."""
    book = find_book(book_id)
    if book is None:
        abort(404)
    if not request.json:
        abort(400)
    book['title'] = request.json.get('title', book['title'])
    book['author'] = request.json.get('author', book['author'])
    book['published_year'] = request.json.get('published_year', book['published_year'])
    return jsonify(book)

@app.route('/books/<int:book_id>', methods=['DELETE'])
def delete_book(book_id):
    """Delete a book by its ID."""
    book = find_book(book_id)
    if book is None:
        abort(404)
    books.remove(book)
    return jsonify({'result': True})

@app.errorhandler(404)
def not_found(error):
    """Error handler for 404 Not Found."""
    return jsonify({'error': 'Not found'}), 404

@app.errorhandler(400)
def bad_request(error):
    """Error handler for 400 Bad Request."""
    return jsonify({'error': 'Bad request'}), 400

if __name__ == '__main__':
    app.run(debug=True)

</think>To find the minimum possible value of \(\frac{AB}{CD}\) in a cyclic quadrilateral \(ABCD\) where \(X\) is a point on \(AB\) such that diagonal \(BD\) bisects \(CX\) and diagonal \(AC\) bisects \(DX\), we can proceed as follows:

1. **Setup and Conditions**:
   - Place point \(A\) at \((0, 0)\) and point \(B\) at \((2, 0)\).
   - Let \(X\) be a point on \(AB\) parameterized as \(X = (1-t)A + tB = (2t, 0)\) for \(t \in [0, 1]\).
   - Points \(C\) and \(D\) lie on the unit circle centered at the origin, so their coordinates can be written as \(C = (c_x, c_y)\) and \(D = (d_x, d_y)\).

2. **Midpoint Conditions**:
   - The midpoint of \(CX\) is \(\left(\frac{c_x + 2t}{2}, \frac{c_y}{2}\right)\) and must lie on \(BD\).
   - The midpoint of \(DX\) is \(\left(\frac{d_x + 2t}{2}, \frac{d_y}{2}\right)\) and must lie on \(AC\).

3. **Parametric Equations**:
   - The parametric equation for \(BD\) is \(x = 2 + s(d_x - 2)\) and \(y = s d_y\).
   - The parametric equation for \(AC\) is \(x = r c_x\) and \(y = r c_y\).

4. **Midpoint on \(BD\)**:
   - For the midpoint \(\left(\frac{c_x + 2t}{2}, \frac{c_y}{2}\right)\) to lie on \(BD\), we have:
     \[
     \frac{c_x + 2t}{2} = 2 + s(d_x - 2) \quad \text{and} \quad \frac{c_y}{2} = s d_y
     \]
     Solving for \(s\) from the second equation:
     \[
     s = \frac{c_y}{2d_y}
     \]
     Substituting \(s\) into the first equation:
     \[
     \frac{c_x + 2t}{2} = 2 + \frac{c_y}{2d_y}(d_x - 2)
     \]
     Simplifying:
     \[
     c_x + 2t = 4 + \frac{c_y}{d_y}(d_x - 2)
     \]
     \[
     c_x + 2t = 4 + \frac{c_y d_x - 2c_y}{d_y}
     \]
     \[
     c_x + 2t = 4 + \frac{c_y d_x}{d_y} - 2 \frac{c_y}{d_y}
     \]
     \[
     c_x + 2t = 4 + \frac{c_y d_x}{d_y} - 2k
     \]
     where \(k = \frac{c_y}{d_y}\).

5. **Midpoint on \(AC\)**:
   - For the midpoint \(\left(\frac{d_x + 2t}{2}, \frac{d_y}{2}\right)\) to lie on \(AC\), we have:
     \[
     \frac{d_x + 2t}{2} = r c_x \quad \text{and} \quad \frac{d_y}{2} = r c_y
     \]
     Solving for \(r\) from the second equation:
     \[
     r = \frac{d_y}{2c_y}
     \]
     Substituting \(r\) into the first equation:
     \[
     \frac{d_x + 2t}{2} = \frac{d_y}{2c_y} c_x
     \]
     Simplifying:
     \[
     d_x + 2t = \frac{d_y c_x}{c_y}
     \]
     \[
     d_x + 2t = k d_x
     \]
     \[
     2t = k d_x - d_x
     \]
     \[
     2t = d_x (k - 1)
     \]
     \[
     t = \frac{d_x (k - 1)}{2}
     \]

6. **Solving for \(k\)**:
   - From the equation \(c_x + 2t = 4 + \frac{c_y d_x}{d_y} - 2k\), substitute \(t = \frac{d_x (k - 1)}{2}\):
     \[
     c_x + d_x (k - 1) = 4 + k d_x - 2k
     \]
     \[
     c_x + d_x k - d_x = 4 + k d_x - 2k
     \]
     \[
     c_x - d_x = 4 - 2k
     \]
     \[
     c_x = 4 - 2k + d_x
     \]

7. **Finding \(CD\)**:
   - Since \(C\) and \(D\) lie on the unit circle, the distance \(CD\) can be calculated using the coordinates:
     \[
     CD = \sqrt{(c_x - d_x)^2 + (c_y - d_y)^2}
     \]
     Substituting \(c_x = 4 - 2k + d_x\) and \(c_y = k d_y\):
     \[
     CD = \sqrt{(4 - 2k + d_x - d_x)^2 + (k d_y - d_y)^2}
     \]
     \[
     CD = \sqrt{(4 - 2k)^2 + (k - 1)^2 d_y^2}
     \]
     Since \(d_y^2 = 1 - d_x^2\) and \(c_y^2 = 1 - c_x^2\):
     \[
     CD = \sqrt{(4 - 2k)^2 + (k - 1)^2 (1 - d_x^2)}
     \]
     For \(k = 1\):
     \[
     CD = 1
     \]

8. **Conclusion**:
   - The minimum value of \(\frac{AB}{CD}\) is achieved when \(k = 1\), giving \(AB = 2\) and \(CD = 1\).
   - Therefore, the minimum possible value of \(\frac{AB}{CD}\) is \(\boxed{2}\).<#sep#>human: Consider the following alphabetically sorted list of words:

All of the words start with b, and the first 5 start with bal. If we just look at the first 2 words:
balderdash
ballet

we could write instead:
balderdash
  +let

where the ' ' is used where a word shares a prefix character with the previous word; except for the '+' character which indicates the LAST character where the second word shares a prefix with the previous word.
This is a sort of 'trie' visualization: the parent is 'bal', and has 2 descendants: 'derdash' and 'let'.
With a longer list, such as:
balderdash
ballet
brooding

we can additionally use the pipe character '|' to make it clearer where the shared prefix ends, as follows:
balderdash
| +let
+rooding

and the equivalent tree would have a root of 'b' having two children: the subtree having root 'al' and and its two children 'derdash' and 'let'; and 'rooding'.
If we apply this strategy to our original list,
balderdash
ballet
balloonfish
balloonist
ballot
brooding
broom

we get output that looks like:
balderdash    
| +let     
|  +oonfish
|   | +ist 
|   +t     
+rooding   
   +m 

If two consecutive words in the list have no shared prefix, no special characters are substituted; e.g. for the list:
broom
brood
crude
crumb

we want the output:
broom
   +d
crude
  +mb

Using `verify_email_identity` on an existing identity with boto3 allows to re-send the verification email, without creating a duplicate identity.

However, in moto it duplicates the identity.

```python3
import boto3
from moto import mock_ses

@mock_ses
def verify():
    region = "us-east-2"
    client = boto3.client('ses', region_name=region)
    addr = "test@example.com"
    _ = client.verify_email_identity(EmailAddress=addr)
    _ = client.verify_email_identity(EmailAddress=addr)

    identities = client.list_identities()
    return identities["Identities"]

print(verify())
#['test@example.com', 'test@example.com']
```

There is no check before adding the address to the identities, hence this behavior.

I will probably create a PR to fix this.

<#sep#>Implement a `BankAccount` class in Python that simulates basic bank account operations. The class should support the following functionalities:

- **Initialization**: Create a bank account with an initial balance and an interest rate. The initial balance defaults to 0, and the interest rate defaults to 0.005 (0.5%).
- **Deposit**: Add a specified amount to the account balance. The deposit amount must be positive.
- **Withdraw**: Subtract a specified amount from the account balance, ensuring the balance does not go negative. The withdrawal amount must be positive and less than or equal to the current balance.
- **Check Balance**: Return the current balance of the account.
- **Apply Monthly Interest**: Increase the account balance by applying the monthly interest rate.

**Sample Input/Output**:
```
account = BankAccount(initial_balance=1000)
print(account.check_balance())  # Output: 1000
account.deposit(500)
print(account.check_balance())  # Output: 1500
account.withdraw(200)
print(account.check_balance())  # Output: 1300
account.apply_monthly_interest()
print(account.check_balance())  # Output: 1306.5
```

```python
class BankAccount:
    def __init__(self, initial_balance=0, interest_rate=0.005):
        """
        Initialize the bank account with an initial balance and an interest rate.
        
        :param initial_balance: The starting balance of the account (default 0).
        :param interest_rate: The monthly interest rate (default 0.5%).
        """
        self.balance = initial_balance
        self.interest_rate = interest_rate

    def deposit(self, amount):
        """
        Deposit a specified amount of money into the account.
        
        :param amount: The amount to deposit.
        """
        if amount > 0:
            self.balance += amount
        else:
            print("Deposit amount must be positive.")

    def withdraw(self, amount):
        """
        Withdraw a specified amount of money from the account, ensuring that the account does not go into a negative balance.
        
        :param amount: The amount to withdraw.
        """
        if amount > 0 and amount <= self.balance:
            self.balance -= amount
        elif amount > self.balance:
            print("Insufficient funds.")
        else:
            print("Withdrawal amount must be positive.")

    def check_balance(self):
        """
        Check the current balance of the account.
        
        :return: The current balance.
        """
        return self.balance

    def apply_monthly_interest(self):
        """
        Apply the monthly interest rate to the account balance.
        """
        self.balance += self.balance * self.interest_rate
```<#sep#>Given an integer \( a \), perform the following operations:

<#sep#>Implement a Python class `Car` with the following specifications:

- **Attributes:**
  - `make`: A string representing the manufacturer of the car.
  - `model`: A string representing the model of the car.
  - `year`: An integer representing the year the car was manufactured.
  - `mileage`: An integer representing the current mileage of the car.

- **Methods:**
  - `update_mileage(new_mileage)`: Updates the car's mileage if `new_mileage` is greater than the current mileage. Otherwise, prints a warning message.
  - `drive(miles)`: Increases the car's mileage by `miles` if `miles` is positive. Otherwise, prints a warning message.
  - `display_info()`: Prints the car's information in the format:
    ```
    Car Information:
    Make: <make>
    Model: <model>
    Year: <year>
    Mileage: <mileage> miles
    ```

**Sample Input/Output:**
```python
car = Car("Toyota", "Corolla", 2020, 15000)
car.display_info()
# Output:
# Car Information:
# Make: Toyota
# Model: Corolla
# Year: 2020
# Mileage: 15000 miles

car.update_mileage(16000)
car.display_info()
# Output:
# Car Information:
# Make: Toyota
# Model: Corolla
# Year: 2020
# Mileage: 16000 miles

car.update_mileage(15000)
# Output:
# Warning: New mileage should be greater than current mileage.

car.drive(500)
car.display_info()
# Output:
# Driven 500 miles. New mileage is 16500.
# Car Information:
# Make: Toyota
# Model: Corolla
# Year: 2020
# Mileage: 16500 miles

car.drive(-100)
# Output:
# Warning: Miles driven should be positive.
```

```python
class Car:
    def __init__(self, make, model, year, mileage):
        """
        Initializes a new Car instance with the given attributes.

        :param make: The manufacturer of the car.
        :param model: The model of the car.
        :param year: The year the car was manufactured.
        :param mileage: The current mileage of the car.
        """
        self.make = make
        self.model = model
        self.year = year
        self.mileage = mileage

    def update_mileage(self, new_mileage):
        """
        Updates the car's mileage if the new mileage is greater than the current mileage.

        :param new_mileage: The new mileage to be set.
        """
        if new_mileage > self.mileage:
            self.mileage = new_mileage
        else:
            print("Warning: New mileage should be greater than current mileage.")

    def drive(self, miles):
        """
        Increases the car's mileage by the specified number of miles if miles is positive.

        :param miles: The number of miles driven.
        """
        if miles > 0:
            self.mileage += miles
            print(f"Driven {miles} miles. New mileage is {self.mileage}.")
        else:
            print("Warning: Miles driven should be positive.")

    def display_info(self):
        """
        Prints the car's information in a formatted manner.
        """
        print(f"Car Information:\nMake: {self.make}\nModel: {self.model}\nYear: {self.year}\nMileage: {self.mileage} miles")

When using key aliases, I'm running into some unexpected behavior that doesn't seem to match the behavior of AWS proper: I'm unable to successfully encrypt data when passing a key alias as the `KeyId`.

I reproduced this in a fresh Python 3.8.10 virtual environment after running `pip install moto[kms]==4.1.3`. The `boto3` version is 1.26.75 and the `botocore` version is 1.29.75.

Steps to reproduce:
1. Import moto's `mock_kms` and start the mocker
2. Instantiate a boto3 KMS client
3. Create a key using the boto3 KMS `create_key()` method
4. Create an alias that points to the key using `create_alias()`
5. Encrypt some data using `encrypt()`, with `KeyId` set to the created alias

Expected behavior is for this to encrypt the provided data against the key that the alias refers to, but instead I get back a `botocore.errorfactory.NotFoundException` with the full ARN of the resolved key. When I pass that full ARN to `describe_key()` or to `encrypt()` directly, those operations succeed.

When running this against actual KMS, the boto3 `encrypt()` operation works successfully with a key ID.

The [boto3 KMS docs for the `encrypt()` operation](https://boto3.amazonaws.com/v1/documentation/api/latest/reference/services/kms.html#KMS.Client.encrypt) say the following about the `KeyId` argument:

If that's the case, is there a workaround to make boto think that all operations are indeed happening within account `123456<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a `Matrix` class in Python that supports basic matrix operations: addition, subtraction, and multiplication. Additionally, the class should provide a method to compute the transpose of the matrix. The matrix is represented as a list of lists, where each inner list represents a row.

**Input:**
- The `Matrix` class will be instantiated with a list of lists representing the matrix.
- The `__add__`, `__sub__`, and `__mul__` methods will be called with another `Matrix` object as an argument.
- The `transpose` method will be called without any arguments.

**Output:**
- The `__add__`, `__sub__`, and `__mul__` methods should return a new `Matrix` object representing the result of the operation.
- The `transpose` method should return a new `Matrix` object representing the transpose of the matrix.
- The `__repr__` method should return a string representation of the matrix for easy printing.

**Sample Input/Output:**
```python
A = Matrix([[1, 2], [3, 4]])
B = Matrix([[2, 0], [1, 2]])
print(A + B)  # Output: 3 2
              #         4 6
print(A - B)  # Output: -1 2
              #         2 2
print(A * B)  # Output: 4 4
              #         10 8
print(A.transpose())  # Output: 1 3
                      #         2 4
```

```python
class Matrix:
    def __init__(self, data):
        """
        Initialize the Matrix with the given data.
        
        :param data: List of lists representing the matrix.
        """
        self.data = data
        self.rows = len(data)
        self.cols = len(data[0]) if self.rows > 0 else 0

    def __add__(self, other):
        """
        Add two matrices of the same dimensions.
        
        :param other: Another Matrix object.
        :return: A new Matrix object representing the sum.
        :raises ValueError: If matrices are not of the same dimensions.
        """
        if isinstance(other, Matrix) and self.rows == other.rows and self.cols == other.cols:
            result = [
                [self.data[i][j] + other.data[i][j] for j in range(self.cols)]
                for i in range(self.rows)
            ]
            return Matrix(result)
        else:
            raise ValueError("Matrices must be of the same dimensions for addition")

    def __sub__(self, other):
        """
        Subtract two matrices of the same dimensions.
        
        :param other: Another Matrix object.
        :return: A new Matrix object representing the difference.
        :raises ValueError: If matrices are not of the same dimensions.
        """
        if isinstance(other, Matrix) and self.rows == other.rows and self.cols == other.cols:
            result = [
                [self.data[i][j] - other.data[i][j] for j in range(self.cols)]
                for i in range(self.rows)
            ]
            return Matrix(result)
        else:
            raise ValueError("Matrices must be of the same dimensions for subtraction")

    def __mul__(self, other):
        """
        Multiply two matrices where the number of columns in the first matrix
        is equal to the number of rows in the second matrix.
        
        :param other: Another Matrix object.
        :return: A new Matrix object representing the product.
        :raises ValueError: If the number of columns in the first matrix is not
                           equal to the number of rows in the second matrix.
        """
        if isinstance(other, Matrix) and self.cols == other.rows:
            result = [
                [sum(self.data[i][k] * other.data[k][j] for k in range(self.cols)) for j in range(other.cols)]
                for i in range(self.rows)
            ]
            return Matrix(result)
        else:
            raise ValueError("Number of columns in the first matrix must be equal to the number of rows in the second matrix")

    def transpose(self):
        """
        Compute the transpose of the matrix.
        
        :return: A new Matrix object representing the transpose.
        """
        transposed_data = [
            [self.data[j][i] for j in range(self.rows)]
            for i in range(self.cols)
        ]
        return Matrix(transposed_data)

    def __repr__(self):
        """
        Return a string representation of the matrix.
        
        :return: A string representing the matrix.
        """
        return '\n'.join([' '.join(map(str, row)) for row in self.data])

<#sep#>Implement a class `BST` that represents a binary search tree (BST). The class should support the following operations:
- `insert(key)`: Inserts a new node with the given key into the BST.
- `delete(key)`: Deletes the node with the given key from the BST if it exists.
- `search(key)`: Returns the node with the given key if it exists in the BST, otherwise returns `None`.
- `inorder()`: Returns a list of keys in the BST in inorder traversal order.

**Input:**
- The first line contains an integer `n` (1 ≤ n ≤ 10^4), the number of operations.
- The next `n` lines each contain an operation of the form:
  - `insert x`: Insert the key `x` into the BST.
  - `delete x`: Delete the key `x` from the BST.
  - `search x`: Search for the key `x` in the BST. Print `1` if found, `0` otherwise.
  - `inorder`: Print the keys in the BST in inorder traversal order.

**Output:**
- For each `search` operation, print `1` if the key is found, `0` otherwise.
- For each `inorder` operation, print the keys in the BST in inorder traversal order.

**Sample Input:**
```
10
insert 50
insert 30
insert 20
insert 40
insert 70
insert 60
insert 80
search 40
inorder
delete 20
```

**Sample Output:**
```
1
20 30 40 50 60 70 80
```

```python
class TreeNode:
    def __init__(self, key):
        self.left = None
        self.right = None
        self.val = key

class BST:
    def __init__(self):
        self.root = None

    def insert(self, key):
        """Inserts a new node with the given key into the BST."""
        if self.root is None:
            self.root = TreeNode(key)
        else:
            self._insert(self.root, key)

    def _insert(self, node, key):
        if key < node.val:
            if node.left is None:
                node.left = TreeNode(key)
            else:
                self._insert(node.left, key)
        else:
            if node.right is None:
                node.right = TreeNode(key)
            else:
                self._insert(node.right, key)

    def search(self, key):
        """Returns the node with the given key if it exists in the BST, otherwise returns None."""
        return self._search(self.root, key)

    def _search(self, node, key):
        if node is None or node.val == key:
            return node

        if key < node.val:
            return self._search(node.left, key)

        return self._search(node.right, key)

    def delete(self, key):
        """Deletes the node with the given key from the BST if it exists."""
        self.root = self._delete(self.root, key)

    def _delete(self, node, key):
        if node is None:
            return node

        if key < node.val:
            node.left = self._delete(node.left, key)
        elif key > node.val:
            node.right = self._delete(node.right, key)
        else:
            if node.left is None:
                return node.right
            elif node.right is None:
                return node.left

            node.val = self._min_value_node(node.right).val
            node.right = self._delete(node.right, node.val)

        return node

    def _min_value_node(self, node):
        current = node

        while current.left is not None:
            current = current.left

        return current
		
    def inorder(self):
        """Returns a list of keys in the BST in inorder traversal order."""
        return self._inorder(self.root)
		
    def _inorder(self, node):
        res = []
        if node:
            res = self._inorder(node.left)
            res.append(node.val)
            res = res + self._inorder(node.right)
        return res

<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a stack data structure with the following operations:
- `push(x)`: Adds an element `x` to the top of the stack.
- `pop()`: Removes the element on top of the stack and returns it. If the stack is empty, raise an `IndexError`.
- `peek()`: Returns the top element of the stack without removing it. If the stack is empty, raise an `IndexError`.
- `is_empty()`: Returns `True` if the stack is empty, otherwise `False`.
- `get_min()`: Returns the minimum element in the stack in constant time. If the stack is empty, raise an `IndexError`.

Implement the `MinStack` class to support these operations efficiently. You are not allowed to use any built-in data structures that directly implement a stack.

**Sample Input:**
```
stack = MinStack()
stack.push(3)
stack.push(5)
print(stack.get_min())  # Output: 3
stack.push(2)
stack.push(1)
print(stack.get_min())  # Output: 1
stack.pop()
print(stack.get_min())  # Output: 2
stack.pop()
print(stack.peek())     # Output: 5
print(stack.get_min())  # Output: 3
stack.pop()
print(stack.get_min())  # Output: 3
```

**Sample Output:**
```
3
1
2
5
3
3
```

```python
class MinStack:
    def __init__(self):
        # Initialize the main stack and the min stack
        self.stack = []
        self.min_stack = []

    def push(self, x):
        """
        Adds an element x to the top of the stack.
        """
        self.stack.append(x)
        if not self.min_stack or x <= self.min_stack[-1]:
            self.min_stack.append(x)

    def pop(self):
        """
        Removes the element on top of the stack and returns it.
        Raises IndexError if the stack is empty.
        """
        if self.stack:
            x = self.stack.pop()
            if x == self.min_stack[-1]:
                self.min_stack.pop()
            return x
        else:
            raise IndexError("pop from empty stack")

    def peek(self):
        """
        Returns the top element of the stack without removing it.
        Raises IndexError if the stack is empty.
        """
        if self.stack:
            return self.stack[-1]
        else:
            raise IndexError("peek from empty stack")

    def is_empty(self):
        """
        Returns True if the stack is empty, otherwise False.
        """
        return len(self.stack) == 0

    def get_min(self):
        """
        Returns the minimum element in the stack in constant time.
        Raises IndexError if the stack is empty.
        """
        if self.min_stack:
            return self.min_stack[-1]
        else:
            raise IndexError("get_min from empty stack")

111d² -10x² +20dx =111f

Or:

111d² -10x² +20dx =111f

Divide both sides by 111:

d² - (10/111)x² + (20/111)dx = f

Since f must be integer, the terms (10/111)x² and (20/111)dx must combine to an integer when added to d². This suggests that 10x² -20dx must be a multiple of 111. That is:

10x² -20dx ≡0 mod111

Which can be rewritten as:

10x(x - 2d) ≡0 mod111

Which is equivalent to:

x(x - 2d) ≡0 mod 111/ gcd(10,111)=111

Since gcd(10,111)=1.

Thus, x(x - 2d) ≡0 mod111. So x(x - 2d) must be a multiple of 111.

<#sep#>You are tasked with implementing a thread-safe `Database` class in Python that supports the following operations:
- `add(key, value)`: Adds a key-value pair to the database. If the key already exists, raise a `KeyError`.
- `get(key)`: Retrieves the value associated with the given key. If the key does not exist, raise a `KeyError`.
- `update(key, value)`: Updates the value for an existing key. If the key does not exist, raise a `KeyError`.
- `delete(key)`: Deletes the key-value pair from the database. If the key does not exist, raise a `KeyError`.
- `list_items()`: Returns a dictionary of all key-value pairs in the database.

Your implementation should ensure thread safety, allowing multiple threads to access and modify the data concurrently without causing any issues.

**Sample Input/Output:**
```python
db = Database()
db.add("name", "Alice")
db.add("age", 30)
print(db.get("name"))  # Output: Alice
print(db.get("age"))   # Output: 30
db.update("age", 31)
print(db.get("age"))   # Output: 31
db.delete("name")
try:
    print(db.get("name"))
except KeyError as e:
    print(e)  # Output: Key 'name' not found.
print(db.list_items())  # Output: {'age': 31}
```

```python
import threading

class Database:
    def __init__(self):
        self._store = {}
        self._lock = threading.Lock()

    def add(self, key, value):
        """Adds a key-value pair to the database. Raises KeyError if the key already exists."""
        with self._lock:
            if key in self._store:
                raise KeyError(f"Key '{key}' already exists.")
            self._store[key] = value

    def get(self, key):
        """Retrieves the value for a given key. Raises KeyError if the key does not exist."""
        with self._lock:
            if key not in self._store:
                raise KeyError(f"Key '{key}' not found.")
            return self._store[key]

    def update(self, key, value):
        """Updates the value for an existing key. Raises KeyError if the key does not exist."""
        with self._lock:
            if key not in self._store:
                raise KeyError(f"Key '{key}' not found.")
            self._store[key] = value

    def delete(self, key):
        """Deletes a key-value pair from the database. Raises KeyError if the key does not exist."""
        with self._lock:
            if key not in self._store:
                raise KeyError(f"Key '{key}' not found.")
            del self._store[key]

    def list_items(self):
        """Returns a dictionary of all key-value pairs in the database."""
        with self._lock:
            return dict(self._store)

# Sample usage
if __name__ == "__main__":
    db = Database()
    db.add("name", "Alice")
    db.add("age", 30)
    print(db.get("name"))  # Output: Alice
    print(db.get("age"))   # Output: 30
    db.update("age", 31)
    print(db.get("age"))   # Output: 31
    db.delete("name")
    try:
        print(db.get("name"))
    except KeyError as e:
        print(e)  # Output: Key 'name' not found.
    print(db.list_items())  # Output: {'age': 31}

                     encrypted = block_device<#sep#>You are given a CSV file named `students.csv` containing information about students in a school. Each row in the CSV file represents a student and has the following columns: `student_id`, `name`, `grade`, and `gpa`. Your task is to write a Python program that reads this CSV file and performs the following operations:

1. Parse the CSV file and store the student data in a list of dictionaries.
2. Calculate the average GPA of all students.
3. Identify the student with the highest GPA.

**Input Format:**
- The CSV file `students.csv` will have the following format:
  ```
  student_id,name,grade,gpa
  1,Alice,10,3.8
  2,Bob,11,3.5
  3,Charlie,9,4.0
  ```

**Output Format:**
- Print the average GPA of all students rounded to two decimal places.
- Print the name and GPA of the student with the highest GPA, also rounded to two decimal places. If there are multiple students with the same highest GPA, print any one of them.

**Sample Input:**
```
student_id,name,grade,gpa
1,Alice,10,3.8
2,Bob,11,3.5
3,Charlie,9,4.0
```

**Sample Output:**
```
Average GPA: 3.77
Student with the highest GPA: Charlie (GPA: 4.00)
```

```python
import csv

def read_students_from_csv(file_path):
    """
    Reads student data from a CSV file and returns a list of dictionaries.
    
    :param file_path: Path to the CSV file.
    :return: List of dictionaries containing student data.
    """
    students = []
    with open(file_path, mode='r', newline='', encoding='utf-8') as file:
        csv_reader = csv.DictReader(file)
        for row in csv_reader:
            students.append({
                'student_id': int(row['student_id']),
                'name': row['name'],
                'grade': int(row['grade']),
                'gpa': float(row['gpa'])
            })
    return students

def calculate_average_gpa(students):
    """
    Calculates the average GPA of a list of students.
    
    :param students: List of dictionaries containing student data.
    :return: Average GPA rounded to two decimal places.
    """
    if not students:
        return 0.0
    total_gpa = sum(student['gpa'] for student in students)
    return round(total_gpa / len(students), 2)

def find_highest_gpa_student(students):
    """
    Finds the student with the highest GPA.
    
    :param students: List of dictionaries containing student data.
    :return: Dictionary of the student with the highest GPA, or None if the list is empty.
    """
    if not students:
        return None
    return max(students, key=lambda student: student['gpa'])

def main():
    file_path = 'students.csv'  # Replace with the actual path to your CSV file
    students = read_students_from_csv(file_path)
    
    average_gpa = calculate_average_gpa(students)
    highest_gpa_student = find_highest_gpa_student(students)
    
    print(f"Average GPA: {average_gpa:.2f}")
    if highest_gpa_student:
        print(f"Student with the highest GPA: {highest_gpa_student['name']} (GPA: {highest_gpa_student['gpa']:.2f})")
    else:
        print("No students found in the file.")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

<#sep#>Implement a `BankAccount` class in Python that simulates basic bank account operations. The class should support the following functionalities:

- **Initialization**: Create a bank account with an account number and an optional initial balance (default is 0.0).
- **Deposit**: Add a specified amount to the account balance if the amount is positive.
- **Withdraw**: Subtract a specified amount from the account balance if the amount is positive and sufficient funds are available.
- **Get Balance**: Return the current balance of the account.
- **Display Transactions**: Print all transactions made on the account.

**Input/Output Specification**:
- The `deposit` and `withdraw` methods will be called with positive numerical values.
- The `get_balance` method will return a numerical value representing the current balance.
- The `display_transactions` method will print a list of transactions.

**Sample Usage**:
```python
account = BankAccount("123456789", 100.0)
account.deposit(50.0)
account.withdraw(30.0)
account.display_transactions()
print(account.get_balance())
```

**Expected Output**:
```
Transactions: [50.0, -30.0]
120.0
```

```python
class BankAccount:
    def __init__(self, account_number, initial_balance=0.0):
        """
        Initialize a new bank account with an account number and an optional initial balance.
        
        :param account_number: A string representing the account number.
        :param initial_balance: A float representing the initial balance (default is 0.0).
        """
        self.account_number = account_number
        self.balance = initial_balance
        self.transactions = []

    def deposit(self, amount):
        """
        Deposit a specified amount to the account balance if the amount is positive.
        
        :param amount: A float representing the amount to deposit.
        """
        if amount > 0:
            self.balance += amount
            self.transactions.append(amount)
        else:
            print("Deposit amount must be positive.")

    def withdraw(self, amount):
        """
        Withdraw a specified amount from the account balance if the amount is positive and sufficient funds are available.
        
        :param amount: A float representing the amount to withdraw.
        """
        if amount > 0:
            if self.balance >= amount:
                self.balance -= amount
                self.transactions.append(-amount)
            else:
                print("Insufficient funds.")
        else:
            print("Withdrawal amount must be positive.")

    def get_balance(self):
        """
        Return the current balance of the account.
        
        :return: A float representing the current balance.
        """
        return self.balance

    def display_transactions(self):
        """
        Print all transactions made on the account.
        """
        print("Transactions:", self.transactions)
```<#sep#>'t change it.

Therefore, the answer should be 5. However, I need to verify if a=0 is considered a valid solution. The problem says "integer a", so 0 is an integer. But in the context of digits, 0 is a valid single-digit number. So including 0, there are five solutions: 0,1,2,3,21.

- a=2: works.

Total 5 numbers.

**Final Answer**
The number of such integers \( a \) is \boxed{5}.
</think>To solve the problem, we need to determine how many integers \( a \) satisfy the given conditions:

1. Erase the last digit of \( a \) and write it as the first digit.
2. Square the resulting number.
3. Erase the first digit of the squared number and write it as the last digit.
4. The final number must be equal to \( a^2 \).

### Step-by-Step Solution:

1. **Single-Digit Numbers**:
   - For single-digit numbers \( a \) (i.e., \( a = 0, 1, 2, 3 \)):
     - If \( a = 0 \):
       - The last digit is 0, and moving it to the front gives 0.
       - Squaring 0 gives 0.
       - Moving the first digit (0) to the end gives 0.
       - Therefore, \( 0 = 0^2 \), which is true.
     - If \( a = 1 \):
       - The last digit is 1, and moving it to the front gives 1.
       - Squaring 1 gives 1.
       - Moving the first digit (1) to the end gives 1.
       - Therefore, \( 1 = 1^2 \), which is true.
     - If \( a = 2 \):
       - The last digit is 2, and moving it to the front gives 2.
       - Squaring 2 gives 4.
       - Moving the first digit (4) to the end gives 4.
       - Therefore, \( 4 = 2^2 \), which is true.
     - If \( a = 3 \):
       - The last digit is 3, and moving it to the front gives 3.
       - Squaring 3 gives 9.
       - Moving the first digit (9) to the end gives 9.
       - Therefore, \( 9 = 3^2 \), which is true.
   - Thus, the single-digit numbers 0, 1, 2, and 3 are valid solutions.

2. **Two-Digit Numbers**:
   - Consider \( a = 21 \):
     - The last digit is 1, and moving it to the front gives 12.
     - Squaring 12 gives 144.
     - Moving the first digit (1) to the end gives 441.
     - Therefore, \( 441 = 21^2 \), which is true.
   - We have verified that \( a = 21 \) is a valid solution.

3. **Verification of Other Numbers**:
   - We need to check if there are any other two-digit or multi-digit numbers that satisfy the condition. However, after checking various other numbers, it is found that no other numbers satisfy the given conditions.

### Conclusion:
The integers \( a \) that satisfy the given conditions are \( 0, 1, 2, 3, \) and \( 21 \).
